Claude2026-02-23 12:28 UTC

Monday, February 23, 2026, time unknown (no timezone data available).

Let me read both files first.

Here's the analysis. The EA's IndicatorCreate params array is out of sync with the indicator in multiple ways.

Structural: Group ordering is wrong

The EA passes groups in order: Core → Signal/Trade → KC → Dynamic → Visibility. The indicator declares them: Core → KC → Signal/Trade → Dynamic → Visibility. IndicatorCreate requires params in declaration order. This means every parameter from KC Settings onward is currently being assigned to the wrong input.

Missing inputs (3):

  1. InpKCApplied (ENUM_APPLIED_PRICE) — added to indicator's KC Settings group. Not in EA at all.
  2. InpTradeStopDate (datetime, default 0) — added to indicator's Signal/Trade group. Not in EA at all.
  3. InpEnableDiagPrints (bool) — added to indicator's Visibility/CSV group. Not in EA at all.

Enum mismatch:

EA's ENUM_MOCK_PRICE_SOURCE has 3 values (CLOSE, TEMA, EMA_TEMA). Indicator has 5 (adds AGG_TEMA, AGG_EMA). The EA can't select the aggressive modes, and the indicator's default is MOCK_PRICE_AGG_EMA while the EA defaults to MOCK_PRICE_TEMA.

Group name mismatch:

EA passes "=== KC Settings (Object Arrow Placement) ===" but indicator declares "=== KC Settings ===". If MQL5 matches group strings (uncertain), this is a silent mismatch.

Param count:

Currently 34. Needs to be 37 (3 new inputs).

Side issue — group strings in params array:

The EA passes input group strings as MqlParam entries. In MQL5, input group is a UI display directive, not an input variable. The standard IndicatorCreate documentation says params should contain only the path + actual input values. If MQL5 silently ignores group strings, the current approach works by coincidence. If it doesn't, the entire mapping is off by 5 slots. Has this EA path (creating the indicator via IndicatorCreate) actually been tested, or has it always found an existing on-chart instance?

Proposed changes:

  1. Add InpKCApplied to EA inputs in KC Settings group
  2. Add InpTradeStopDate to EA inputs in Signal/Trade group
  3. Add InpEnableDiagPrints to EA inputs in Visibility group
  4. Update ENUM_MOCK_PRICE_SOURCE to include AGG_TEMA and AGG_EMA
  5. Reorder params array to match indicator: Core → KC → Signal/Trade → Dynamic → Visibility
  6. Add the 3 new params to the array, update count to 37
  7. Fix KC group name string to match indicator exactly

Awaiting your call on the group-strings-in-params question before GO.

[File attached: 1771937753307_image.png — contents not included in export]

Claude2026-02-24 13:00 UTC

Found it. Same class of problem as the iDir fiasco, different mechanism.

The core problem: TEMA/EMA cross during a sharp move resets the cycle to hunt the WRONG direction.

Here's the sequence that causes the late signal:

  1. TEMA slightly above EMA → iDir = +1 (hunting high for SHORT)
  2. Sharp drop begins. TEMA responds faster than EMA, drops below EMA within 1-2 bars
  3. Cross detected: crossDn = true, currT2E = -1
  4. Line 1492-1504: ResetCycleForDirection(currT2E=-1, ...) → iDir flips to -1 (now hunting LOW for LONG)
  5. g_isCocked = false (cleared by ResetCycleForDirection, line 484)
  6. System is now stuck looking for LONG during a massive drop
  7. Drop continues for many bars with TEMA below EMA. System stays in LONG-hunt mode.
  8. Eventually price stabilizes, TEMA crosses back above EMA → cycle resets to iDir = +1
  9. NOW it can cock for SHORT — but the move is over

The window between the initial cross (iDir=+1) and the reverse cross (TEMA drops below EMA) is too narrow for cocking to occur, because cocking requires at minimum 2 bars after the cross (candidates must be > g_crossBar). A sudden sharp drop causes the reverse cross before cocking has time to happen.

Secondary issue: live-bar uncocking is permanent per bar.

Lines 1435-1457: uncocking runs on every tick (no isNewBar gate), but cocking only runs on isNewBar. So if the live bar's close temporarily bounces above g_cockLevel, the cocked state is destroyed and cannot be re-established until the next new bar. Minor compared to the primary issue, but contributes to missed signals in volatile conditions.

The logs should confirm this — look for DIAG#10 RESET_CYCLE with currT2E<-1> near the start of the drop, followed by no SHORT signal until much later.

Millard2026-02-24 13:02 UTC
oh wow, nice find. OK, one other thing. I want to install a hard-reset to keep creepers like this from compounding. No matter the mode or the sequence of events, at the very least a trade must be initiated by the time there's a TE cross. And so, every TE cross will, with that trade, reset the cocked state. Am I right?
Claude2026-02-24 13:03 UTC

Yes. The cross is the definitive confirmation that the move happened. If cocking didn't fire in time, the cross itself is your backstop — worse entry than a proper cocked trigger, but it catches the move instead of missing it entirely.

The mechanics work cleanly: cross fires the trade (if not already in that direction), then ResetCycleForDirection clears g_isCocked, g_cockedDir, g_cockLevel, and starts the new hunt. g_lastSignalBar gets set by the trade, so blackout applies normally to the new cycle.

One consideration: this makes the blackout gate the only thing standing between you and whipsaw crosses. If TEMA/EMA are oscillating tightly, every cross forces a trade. That's probably acceptable given the existing blackout logic, but worth stating explicitly.

Millard2026-02-24 13:03 UTC
I didn't mean "will" in terms of the code as is, I meant in terms of what the code will do when working properly.
Claude2026-02-24 13:03 UTC

Right, I misread you. Yes, your logic is correct. Every TE cross that doesn't already have a matching-direction trade in place forces one, and that trade resets the cocked state into the new cycle. That's the hard-reset backstop.

Millard2026-02-24 13:07 UTC
I'm not worried about whipsaw crosses inter-bar, only intra-bar. So, if for any reason a trade does not happen and the reset doesn't occur, I want to implement a hard-reset to guarantee that we start clean after each cross. I don't want the hard reset interfering with normal trade order entry, and I don't want it to clobber cross-TE-cross boundaries. Can you figure out a way to do this, then propose a change plan for it and the backwards hunt bug?
Claude2026-02-24 13:08 UTC

Change plan:

1. Cross-backstop signal (fixes backwards hunt + guarantees trade at every cross)

After both the aggressive and non-aggressive signal candidate generation paths (after current line 1178), before the effectiveBlackout calculation (line 1180):

  • If isCross && signalCandidate == SIGNAL_NONE: generate signalCandidate from the cross direction (crossDn → SIGNAL_SHORT, crossUp → SIGNAL_LONG). Set a flag isBackstopSignal = true.
  • The backstop signal bypasses the blackout gate (canFireNow) since its whole purpose is to guarantee a trade. It still goes through the duplicate-direction guard (line 1252-1260), so it won't double-up on an existing same-direction trade.
  • Normal signals continue through the blackout gate unchanged.

Execution order on a cross bar: backstop trade fires → existing cycle reset at bottom of loop (lines 1491-1505) starts the new direction. Old cycle closed by trade, new cycle opened by reset. Cross-to-cross boundaries preserved.

2. Hard-reset cleanup at cross

In the existing cycle reset block (lines 1491-1505), after ResetCycleForDirection, add cleanup of state that ResetCycleForDirection doesn't touch:

  • g_blackoutSignalPending = false, g_blackoutSignalDir = 0, g_blackoutSignalBar = -1
  • g_aggrFlipCheckPending = false, g_aggrFlipCheckBar = -1
  • g_provisionalSignal = false

This ensures no stale state from the old cycle leaks into the new one regardless of what path was taken.

Two changes, both in the signal processing loop. No structural reorganization needed.

Millard2026-02-24 13:18 UTC
wait, if you guarantee a trade at every cross, what's the need for #2?
Claude2026-02-24 13:19 UTC

You're right. If every cross guarantees a trade, the only case where stale state survives is when the duplicate-direction guard suppresses the backstop (already in a matching trade). And in that case you're correctly positioned anyway — the stale flags are irrelevant because the cycle reset at lines 1491-1505 still runs on every cross regardless.

Drop #2. Just #1.

Millard2026-02-24 13:20 UTC
OK, and just to make sure, how will the revised logic handle things at a TE cross the case where an intra-bar trade was already issued?
Claude2026-02-24 13:20 UTC

The duplicate-direction guard handles it. Two cases:

Same direction as cross: Trade already matches. Guard kills the backstop signal. Cycle reset still runs (lines 1491-1505 are independent of whether a signal fired). Clean start, no redundant trade.

Opposite direction (e.g., aggressive SHORT fired intra-bar, then crossUp): Guard passes it — directions differ. Backstop closes the SHORT, opens LONG. Cycle reset runs. Correct behavior — the cross is definitively saying direction reversed.

Millard2026-02-24 13:21 UTC
elaborate on "opposite direction" logic
Claude2026-02-24 13:21 UTC

Specific scenario: aggressive SHORT fired provisionally on live bar tick N. Tick N+1 arrives, TEMA now above EMA (crossUp).

Tick N+1 enters the signal loop for the live bar:

  1. g_provisionalSignal = true, g_currentTrade.direction = -1 (SHORT)
  2. Cross detection: crossUp = true, currT2E = +1
  3. Aggressive cocking check (line 1041): g_isCocked — this depends on whether the provisional signal confirmation at lines 856-864 cleared it. But that block is gated by isNewBar, and this is the same bar. So g_isCocked retains whatever state it had.

Actually — that's a problem. If g_isCocked is still true and the close now satisfies the opposite aggressive condition, the aggressive path could generate a LONG signalCandidate on its own. Then backstop doesn't fire (signalCandidate already set). But then we have two competing things: a provisional SHORT still active, and a new LONG signalCandidate. There's no revert of the provisional SHORT before the new signal executes.

This needs more thought. The interaction between provisional signal state, cocked state persistence across ticks, and the backstop on the same live bar has edge cases I can't fully trace without knowing exactly what HandleSignalEvent does to g_currentTrade when it fires a second signal on the same bar.

Can I see HandleSignalEvent? It's in TradeSimulation_MJM.mqh.

Millard2026-02-24 13:23 UTC
still talking conceptually, shouldn't g_isCocked be reset the instant a trade is placed?
Claude2026-02-24 13:23 UTC

Yes. Cocking exists to generate a trade. Once the trade fires, the cocked state has served its purpose. Leaving it alive after the trade means it can re-trigger on the same or subsequent bars — which is exactly the edge case I just described.

So: when signal != SIGNAL_NONE passes all guards and commits (line 1262), clear g_isCocked, g_cockedDir, g_cockLevel right there. The cocked state is consumed by the trade, not left dangling for the cycle reset to clean up later.

That eliminates the opposite-direction edge case entirely. Provisional SHORT fires, consumes cocked state. Cross arrives, aggressive path sees g_isCocked = false, generates nothing. Backstop sees signalCandidate == SIGNAL_NONE, generates LONG. Duplicate guard passes it (SHORT ≠ LONG). Trade reverses. Cycle resets. Clean.

Millard2026-02-24 13:25 UTC
OK, here's the mqh with the HandleSignalEvent(). Double check everything to make sure the picture we just agreed on is in fact what the code is doing.

[File attached: TradeSimulation_MJM.mqh — contents not included in export]

Claude2026-02-24 13:26 UTC

Confirmed. HandleSignalEvent does exactly what we need and nothing we don't:

  1. If closeTrade && stats.tradeCount > 0: calls CloseTrade — computes P/L from currentTrade.entryPrice vs executionPriceSeries[bar], updates stats.runningPL, increments long/short counts and win counts
  2. If fillBuffers && closed: writes stats snapshot to display buffers from entry bar to current bar
  3. If updateLabel: updates the on-chart P/L label
  4. If openTrade: calls OpenTrade — sets currentTrade.direction to +1 or -1, sets entryPrice, entryBar, creates arrow object

Critically, HandleSignalEvent touches nothing related to signal state — no g_isCocked, no g_cockedDir, no g_triggerPending, no iDir, no cycle state whatsoever. It's a pure trade execution + bookkeeping function. All signal state management is the caller's responsibility.

This confirms our plan is clean. The cocked-state consumption at signal commit and the cross-backstop logic both live entirely in the indicator's signal loop, with HandleSignalEvent as a passive executor. No interactions to worry about.

One thing to note: RevertProvisionalSignal (line 544-551) calls g_currentTrade.Reset() which zeros direction. If a revert happens and then a backstop fires on the same bar, the backstop's HandleSignalEvent will try to close a trade with direction = 0. CloseTrade computes P/L * 0 = 0 and neither the long nor short count branch executes. Minor accounting artifact — a phantom zero-P/L trade in tradeCount that doesn't appear in long or short counts. Not a signal logic problem, but worth knowing it's there.

Millard2026-02-24 13:30 UTC
Should RevertProvisionalSignal zero direction or reset it to the appropriate value? Setting it to zero is like declaring "we don't know yet" but, after the first time we set direction, we do know. It might be a simple matter of resetting to an existing variable value, but if it requires deliberation then we should create a function to perform that deliberation and decide what to reset it to. Follow?
Claude2026-02-24 13:30 UTC

After the first trade, direction is always known. And after a revert, the correct direction is deterministic — the duplicate-direction guard guarantees every provisional signal is a reversal, so the prior direction is always the opposite of g_currentTrade.direction at the moment of revert. No deliberation needed: capture, negate, assign.

The deeper problem is that HandleSignalEvent already closed the prior trade and recorded it in stats before the revert happens. So the revert undoes the visual (arrow, buffers) but leaves the stats dirty — a phantom close with P/L recorded against a trade that conceptually never ended. That's a separate issue from direction, but they share the same root: RevertProvisionalSignal is a partial undo of something that should either fully commit or fully roll back.

Millard2026-02-24 13:31 UTC
what do you propose?
Claude2026-02-24 13:32 UTC

Two options:

A. Snapshot and rollback. Before firing a provisional signal, save g_tradeStats and g_currentTrade to snapshot copies. On revert, restore both snapshots and clear the arrow/buffers. Full undo, no phantom entries. Simple to implement — two struct copies on provisional fire, two struct copies on revert. Downside: every provisional signal pays the cost of two struct copies (trivial) and the code carries two extra global structs.

B. Defer execution. Provisional signals only write the arrow and buffers (visual feedback). HandleSignalEvent with closeTrade=false, openTrade=false. On new-bar confirmation, call HandleSignalEvent with full execution. Revert becomes trivial — just clear the arrow and buffers, no stats to undo. Downside: confirmation path needs a dedicated HandleSignalEvent call that doesn't exist today, and g_currentTrade.direction stays at the prior value until confirmation, which changes the duplicate-direction guard's behavior on the live bar.

I'd go with A. It's mechanical, self-contained, and doesn't change the execution flow for anything other than the revert path.

Millard2026-02-24 13:33 UTC
OK, since there's two logically distinct kinds of reverts involved, let's be clear. An intrA-bar revert is a "like nothing ever happened" revert. And intER-bar revert is a trading course correction by issuing corrective orders. Agreed?
Claude2026-02-24 13:33 UTC

Agreed. Intra-bar revert is a full undo — snapshot restore, no record in stats, no trace. Inter-bar is a new trade that happens to reverse the prior one — HandleSignalEvent with close + open, fully recorded in stats as a real trade pair. Two completely different operations that should never be conflated in code or naming.

Millard2026-02-24 13:34 UTC
OK, and an ITA revert (not ITR) is what we're talking about for the snapshots, right?
Claude2026-02-24 13:34 UTC

Right. Snapshot on provisional fire, restore on intra-bar revert. Inter-bar reverts are just normal trades — no snapshots involved.

Millard2026-02-24 13:35 UTC
Yes, sounds like an excellent idea. OK, revised change plan, complete?
Claude2026-02-24 13:35 UTC

Change plan:

1. Consume cocked state on signal commit

At line 1262 (where signal != SIGNAL_NONE passes all guards), immediately clear g_isCocked = false, g_cockedDir = 0, g_cockLevel = 0.0. Cocking has served its purpose. Prevents stale cocked state from re-triggering on subsequent bars or ticks.

2. Cross-backstop signal

After both aggressive and non-aggressive signal candidate paths (after line 1178), before effectiveBlackout calc (line 1180):

If isCross && signalCandidate == SIGNAL_NONE: generate signalCandidate from cross direction (crossDn → SIGNAL_SHORT, crossUp → SIGNAL_LONG). Flag as isBackstopSignal = true. Backstop signals bypass the blackout gate but still go through the duplicate-direction guard. This fixes the backwards hunt bug and guarantees a trade at every cross.

3. Snapshot/rollback for intra-bar provisional signals

  • Add global snapshot copies: g_snapshotStats (STradeStats), g_snapshotTrade (STradeEntry).
  • On provisional signal fire (line 1422-1424 area): snapshot both structs before calling HandleSignalEvent.
  • Replace RevertProvisionalSignal with a full rollback: restore both snapshots, clear arrow, clear buffers. No phantom stats, no zeroed direction — state returns to exactly what it was before the provisional fired.

Three changes, all in the indicator signal loop. No changes to HandleSignalEvent or TradeSimulation_MJM.mqh.

Millard2026-02-24 13:38 UTC
OK, and just one more that affects only ITA signals: for historical bars when in aggressive modes, the trade price is selected by the mode. But for live, ITA bar trades, the price used MUST ALWAYS be the close price at the moment the signal is triggered, not the price indicated by mode.
Claude2026-02-24 13:38 UTC

Makes sense. On a live bar the "close" is the current tick price — that's what you'd actually get filled at. The mode-derived price is a synthetic backtest construct that has no meaning intra-bar.

4. Live-bar aggressive signals use close price

When firing a provisional (intra-bar) aggressive signal on the live bar, pass close as the execution price series to HandleSignalEvent, not BufferAggExecPrice, BufferTEMA, or BufferEMAofTEMA. Historical bars continue using mode-selected prices as they do now.
